	*comment New Lucca scene at St. Anthony of Padua. Begins Lucca RO track.
	St. Anthony of Padua is a thin wedge of a building, its light brown brick façade overseeing the intersection between Gladstone Avenue and Booth Street. A wide set of steps leads up to the entrance, separated off into three even rows by a set of black metal railings. To their right, a white van is parked in the cramped driveway of a delivery bay. Its engine is off and the driver is nowhere to be seen. You search for signs of movement, but the only mortals anywhere near the church are two very large men, seated in metal chairs across the street as they smoke cigarettes in the small Piazza Dante park. They don't seem to be looking at you.
	
	"That's one of the vans I saw the other night," Lucca whispers. "There are two more just like it that come later. Unmarked."

	"You're sure that this isn't some normal operation?" you ask. "It looks like a standard van. Maybe they just get their deliveries at night?"
	
	Lucca shakes her head. "I can't tell you how I know…I just do. And if I'm wrong, we can laugh about it another night and I'll still owe you a favor. That's fair, right?"
	
	"Okay, but there aren't many places to hide around here," you say. "How are we supposed to watch them without looking suspicious?
	
	*if ((obfuscate) or (oblivion))
		"Do you even need to ask?" Lucca says as she waves you toward the delivery bay and melds with the shadows. A moment later you join her, swathed in darkness, silent and complete.

	*else
		"We just have to time it right," Lucca says. "Just keep walking past like we have every reason to be here. Nothing unusual, just a late evening stroll." 
		
		The two men across the street crane their necks to watch as you walk past, but quickly return to their muttered conversation when they realize you're not hanging around. Lucca alters her course immediately and you follow her into the shadows of the building beside the church. You pause for a moment, watching for any sign that you've been spotted. Neither of the men move and no one raises a shout of alarm. "Follow me," Lucca whispers as she makes her way carefully toward the delivery bay. If it had been day time, the two of you would look ludicrous, but at night the road lamps cast cones of light along the street, leaving the building fronts significantly darker by comparison. It's not perfect shadow, but it's workable for what you need.

You're fairly well obscured behind several shrubs, and so far no one has turned an eye in your direction. After several trips, the men run out of cargo. Instead of bringing more coolers in, they start lugging out heavy-looking oblong crates, taking them from the church and shoving them into the back of the van one at a time. They almost look like…

	*label see_ritual_with_lucca			
	Three parishioners kneel before an altar veiled by a delicate black fabric bearing the symbol of a stylized crown. You could swear you've seen it before but can't quite place it. You see no sign of whoever had cried out, and the praying mortals don't seem to have noticed or been bothered by it enough to be moved from their supplications. Lucca hisses when she sees the banner, but she says nothing, only watches intently as a tall, thin man in priest's robes approaches the altar. His vestments have been dyed a bright royal purple, emblazoned with dazzlingly intricate white embroidery. He greets his flock with a predatory smile, eyes flashing in the candlelight with silent yearning.
	
	"Father Moretti," the woman in the center of the trio says as she lowers her head ever further, as though she dare not gaze into the priest's eyes. "Will you bless us, tonight?" The request is chased by an eerie moaning from the other two kneeling parishioners. Your senses catch fire with a bestial desire. The smell of blood radiates from Moretti in invisible waves, leaving you momentarily stunned.

	You're starting to get a pretty good idea of what's going on here. 
	*page_break
	You start to creep forward to get a better look, but Lucca puts a warning hand on your shoulder. "We have to be sure," she whispers. "You work in Corliss's office. Have you seen any reports of visiting Kindred matching this description?"
	
	You shake your head. "No. And like you said earlier, he's been here for some time now, taking over for the priest you were close with."
	
	Lucca pulls a smartphone from her pocket. She's not supposed to have one, of course, but you're not entirely surprised. Technically it's only internet communications that Arundel sought to avoid with his ban. "I knew they were invading," she seethes near-silently through clenched teeth. "I just didn't know they were already entrenched." She starts filming just as 'Father Moretti' sinks his teeth into the mortal woman's neck and drinks in slow, delicate sips.
	
	He pulls back, red-stained lips stretched thin in the same chilling smile. "Blessed are you, my child." He hands her a Eucharist wafer and she clutches the small piece of bread with holy reverence. Her hands tremble. Only when she brings the offering to her lips do you notice the blood soaked into the dry crust. "The three of you are my heralds," Moretti says. "Go forth and recruit more children for the flock. Together we will grow strong again."

Alisha Grey's exhibition hall has been newly rebuilt, restructured from the ground-up as a monument to artistic extravagance. Its exterior wall is masterfully crafted with layered strata of subtly varied, roughly hewn stone bricks framing glass display windows that hint at the wonders to be found within. The effect is rather lessened by a nearly twenty-foot tall banner proclaiming "This Week Only! Canada's Finest Modern Art!" As the exhibition begins, you find yourself in the midst of a who's-who of Ottawa's rich and powerful. There is a wider variety of Kindred at the gathering this time, with the notable exceptions of your sire, Henrik Lang, and Vivian Maier. Newcomers whom you'd only met once or twice before are younger—less powerful than their Primogen counterparts who took place in yesterday's council meeting—but no less influential in the world of mortal politics and business. Some of these younger vampires gawk in naked awe of their elders, while others put on an equally revealing front of indifference to the centuries of collective power separating them from their betters. 

Soothing tones of inoffensively banal music ooze through the silver grilles of speakers at all four corners of the mezzanine as a small throng of men in suits haggle over a deal with a shrewd-looking businesswoman you recognize as a local Malkavian. It's quite a lot to take in at once.
